_source line 4566 · 2026-05-21T10:58:08.423291+00:00_

16	Distributional compression under recursive generation is well-documented \cite{curious_decline,broken_telephone,markovian_chains}.
17	We introduce \emph{depth fingerprinting}, classifying text by ordinal generation depth (gen-0\,/\,gen-1\,/\,gen-2+)---and decompose this compression into structurally distinct components: across eight base-model generators spanning five architectural families (0.6B--8B), the NLL distribution's central tendency (mean entropy, lexical diversity) drifts monotonically with depth while its shape (entropy extremes, tail structure) remains an order of magnitude more stable ($d{=}0.76$--$1.17$ vs.\ $d < 0.10$; convergence across parametric and non-parametric effect-size measures confirms this partition).
18	We term this location/shape asymmetry the \emph{differential depth response} (DDR; Figure~\ref{fig:feature-drift}), a structural property of fixed-parameter autoregressive sampling that is mechanistically distinct from the tail-coverage erosion in model collapse \cite{curse_of_recursion} and analytically informative independent of both the specific mechanism and classification accuracy.

_source line 4570 · 2026-05-21T10:58:15.908945+00:00_

18	Two concurrent works are most directly relevant: \citet{broken_telephone} analyze paraphrasing and translation chains, documenting progressive information loss, while \citet{markovian_chains} demonstrate attractor dynamics that bound distinguishable depth to approximately three classes; \citet{attractor_cycles} provide complementary evidence that LLM outputs converge to cyclic attractor states under repeated generation.
19	We study \emph{non-paraphrastic continuation} chains, where the absence of semantic anchoring permits richer depth signal, and offer a complementary decomposition: where these works characterize \emph{that} and \emph{why} convergence occurs, DDR isolates \emph{what} converges (NLL location) from what is preserved (NLL shape), enabling ordinal classification without per-generator calibration.
20	The independent convergence of all three approaches on ${\sim}$3 distinguishable depth classes---via Markovian dynamics \cite{markovian_chains}, information-theoretic loss \cite{broken_telephone}, and distributional feature compression (this work)---points to a shared resolution ceiling in recursive text generation (Table~\ref{tab:iterative-comparison} in Appendix~\ref{app:iterative-comparison} provides a structured comparison).
21	
22	\subsection{Vocabulary Diversity and Stylometry}

_source line 4574 · 2026-05-21T10:58:24.861316+00:00_

8	RAID \cite{raid} benchmarks binary detection across generators and attacks; we extend the task from binary to ordinal depth estimation.
9	Several recent efforts move beyond the binary boundary---collaboration roles \cite{beyond_binary}, joint detection-attribution \cite{two_birds}, creator-editor modes \cite{beyond_final_actor}, multi-generator attribution \cite{semeval2024_task8}---but all taxonomize by \emph{source} or \emph{production mode}, not recursive generation \emph{depth}; repurposing their scores as depth predictors reveals high cross-generator variance (binary F1\,=\,0.52--0.71; Table~\ref{tab:baselines}) \cite{paraphrase_evades,detection_possibilities}.
10	Our contribution is orthogonal: ordinal depth classification \cite{gutierrez_ordinal_survey,diaz_soft_labels_ordinal,coral_ordinal,kasa_ordinality_nlp} as a task, and the finding that combining drifting and stationary features yields consistent cross-generator performance where individual detection scores show high variance \cite{uchendu_attribution,munir_looking_glass}.

[Director 指令] **第二轮深度 Writing 优化 — Narrative Flow + 句式结构 + 学术语气**

第一轮完成了 em-dash 修复和 Contributions 重构。第二轮深入优化以下方面：

**1. Narrative Flow 优化**
- 检查段间过渡是否自然。每段结尾应 set up 下一段的主题（"problem → gap → our approach → evidence"）
- Introduction 是否有 elimination ladder 递进感：第一段（binary detection 不够）→ 第二段（concurrent work 只看 what/why converges）→ 第三段（DDR 看 what is preserved）→ contributions
- Results §4 是否遵循 evidence → results → robustness → ablation narrative arc

**2. 句式结构**
- 避免连续 3 个以上短句或连续 2 个以上长句（节奏感）
- 检查关键 claim 句是否足够 assertive（"We find that..." 比 "It can be observed that..." 强）
- 将 "we show / we demonstrate / we find" 的重复使用多样化
- 段首句应是该段核心 claim，后续句提供 evidence

**3. 学术语气升级**
- 去除残余 hedging 语气（"seems to", "appears to", "might"）除非确实需要 hedge
- 用 precise 的学术动词：establish, demonstrate, confirm, reveal（而非 show, find, get）
- 避免 "Interestingly" / "Notably" / "It is worth noting" 等 filler
- Negative results 用 confident 的 neutral 语气呈现（"X does not improve Y" 而非 "X fails to improve Y"）

**4. Abstract 精炼**
- Abstract 每个句子都应 carry unique information。检查是否有冗余或重复的信息
- 最后一句 scope limitation 是否太弱？"The signal attenuates under instruction tuning; current applicability is restricted to base models." 可以更有力

**硬约束不变：** 主文 8 页、不改数据/数字/claim、等长或更短替换。docs/paper/ 和 artifacts/_project/paper/ 双向同步。不需要验证引用。

_source line 4602 · 2026-05-21T11:15:01.117957+00:00_

1	% Abstract — Depth Fingerprinting of Recursive LLM Generation Chains
2	\begin{abstract}
3	When base-model LLM outputs re-enter generation pipelines, each recursive step compresses \emph{where} text sits in distributional space while leaving \emph{how} it is shaped nearly unchanged.
4	We identify this asymmetry as \emph{differential depth response} (DDR) and characterize it across eight \textbf{base-model} generators spanning five architectural families (0.6B--8B parameters): under nucleus sampling ($p{=}0.9$, $\tau \leq 0.7$), NLL location statistics and lexical diversity drift monotonically with generation depth (Cohen's $d{=}0.76$--$1.17$), while distributional shape features remain near-stationary ($d < 0.10$), a separation confirmed by convergence across parametric and non-parametric effect-size measures and clustering-based validation (silhouette\,=\,0.660).
5	Where prior work on iterative generation documents \emph{what converges}, the novel observation here is \emph{what is preserved}: shape invariance under the same process that compresses location provides the stable reference needed for depth estimation without per-generator calibration.
6	As a proof of concept, a lightweight 7-feature ensemble exploiting DDR achieves leak-free binary F1\,=\,0.694 (gen-1 vs.\ gen-2+, effective resolution: three depth classes) under leave-one-generator-out evaluation, surpassing individual baselines and matching multi-detector ensembles at $2.4\times$ lower cost; an end-to-end classifier recovers substantially more (F1\,=\,0.905, 6-gen LOGO), establishing the analytical framework, not the specific features, as the primary contribution.
7	The signal attenuates under instruction tuning; current applicability is restricted to base models.
8	\end{abstract}
9

1	\section{Introduction}
2	\label{sec:introduction}
3	
4	AI text detection asks a single binary question, \emph{human or machine}, and recent methods answer it with increasing reliability \cite{detectgpt,fast_detectgpt,binoculars,ld_score}.
5	Yet this framing elides a richer structure emerging on the open web: as LLM outputs re-enter training corpora \cite{dolma,fineweb} and seed further generation, text accumulates at varying \emph{recursive depths}: an estimated 7--17\% of recent scientific publications already contain substantial LLM-modified content \cite{mapping_llm_science,monitoring_ai_review}, and each generation cycle pushes distributional properties further from both the human source and the first-generation predecessor.
6	While most deployed LLM outputs use instruction-tuned models, base-model generation pipelines remain prevalent in synthetic data production, pre-training corpus construction, and recursive data augmentation; we accordingly scope this study to base (pretrained) models.
7	Knowing \emph{how many times} text has been recursively generated matters for data provenance \cite{ccnet,dsir} and model collapse analysis \cite{curse_of_recursion,strong_model_collapse}, yet no current method moves beyond the binary boundary.
8	
9	\begin{figure}[t!]
10	\centering
11	\includegraphics[width=\columnwidth]{fig1_differential_response.pdf}
12	\caption{Differential depth response (DDR). Diamonds: cross-generator means; dots: per-generator values. Drifting features (blue; $|\rho| > 0.60$, $d{=}0.76$--$1.17$) show strong monotonic depth sensitivity; non-drifting features remain stable. Entropy std (wine; $d{=}0.55$) is an intermediate member; non-drifting features (amber; $d < 0.10$) show negligible drift. Entropy min ($|\rho|{=}0.40$ pooled; gen-0/gen-1 sensitive) and tail decay (TOST 4/8) are boundary features within this group.}
13	\label{fig:feature-drift}
14	\end{figure}
15

1	\section{Results}
2	\label{sec:results}
3	
4	\subsection{Depth Classification Performance}
5	\label{sec:results-classification}
6	
7	The 7-feature ensemble achieves 3-class LOGO F1\,=\,0.762 (95\% CI [0.758, 0.766]; $N$\,=\,37{,}058) across eight generators from five families (Table~\ref{tab:logo-results}), with cross-generator F1 spread of 9.1\,pp capturing generator selection uncertainty.
8	Because gen-0 seeds are shared across generators (\S\ref{sec:generation}), gen-0 classification is near-perfect (per-class F1\,$\approx$\,0.94), inflating the 3-class metric.
9	We therefore adopt \textbf{leak-free binary F1} (gen-1 vs.\ gen-2+)\,=\,0.694 (CI [0.688, 0.700]) as the primary metric throughout: it excludes gen-0 entirely and is unaffected by seed sharing.
10	Per-generator binary F1 ranges from 0.633 (Mistral-7B) to 0.724 (Pythia-2.8B; SD\,=\,0.028), with the primary difficulty concentrated at the gen-1\,/\,gen-2+ boundary (Appendix~\ref{app:confusion}).
11	Note that CIs above reflect bootstrap sampling variability; the cross-generator range (9.1\,pp) captures the distinct and larger uncertainty of generator selection.
12	
13	\paragraph{Cross-model generalization.}
14	Cross-model chains (randomly selected generator per step) yield binary F1\,=\,0.830 (95\% CI [.818, .841]) under 5-generator LOGO.\footnote{5-generator subset (Pythia-2.8B/1B, OLMo-2-1B, Qwen3-0.6B/1.7B); generator-level LOGO with bootstrap CIs ($n$\,=\,2{,}000). Not directly comparable to 8-generator LOGO due to differing subsets.}
15	Single-generator transfer (Appendix~\ref{app:transfer}) confirms the signal is not a mixed-chain artifact: classifiers trained on one generator transfer to all others with mean binary F1\,=\,0.810; LOFO yields 0.686.
16	A scale effect emerges: Pythia achieves the highest F1 (0.718--0.724) while larger models degrade (Mistral-7B: 0.633), partly reflecting same-family affinity with the Pythia-410M reference (cross-reference spread $\leq$0.013; \S\ref{sec:results-differen

1	\section{Discussion}
2	\label{sec:discussion}
3	
4	Recursive generation alters \emph{where} the token-level surprise distribution sits but not \emph{how} it is shaped, a dichotomy with a mechanistic basis.
5	Under nucleus sampling, top-$p$ truncation preserves the functional form of the conditional distribution at each step; what changes is the \emph{input}: as the model conditions on increasingly self-typical outputs, expected NLL compresses monotonically.
6	This yields two testable predictions: (1)~increasing sampling randomness should weaken the location drift, and (2)~semantic-preservation constraints should attenuate it.
7	Both are confirmed: at $t{=}1.0$, location signal collapses ($\rho$: $-0.60 \to -0.002$; Appendix~\ref{app:sampling-sensitivity}); under elaboration prompts, drift weakens (F1\,=\,0.730 vs.\ 0.781; Appendix~\ref{app:prompt-type}).
8	Causal analyses provide supporting evidence: drifting features contribute $5.5\times$ more to classification, with 6/7 exhibiting significant Granger causality over the five-point chain, though the short series length (5 depths) limits formal causal inference (Appendix~\ref{app:ddr-causality}).
9	These results are established under nucleus sampling ($p{=}0.95$); shape features remain stable even at $\tau{=}1.0$ ($d < 0.15$; Appendix~\ref{app:ddr-temp-stability}), but verification under greedy and top-$k$ decoding remains open.
10	
11	The classifier is a proof-of-concept for corpus-level auditing, not per-document filtering: gen-1 per-class F1\,=\,0.63 is insufficient for individual flagging but supports batch-level contamination estimation (${\pm}3$--$4$\% at $n{=}1{,}000$; Appendix~\ref{app:error-analysis}).
12	The three-class resolution enables depth-stratified corpus curation (retaining gen-1 content while flagging recursively degraded material), a distinction invisible to binary classifiers.
13	Depth-aware filtering can serve as a prophylactic against model collapse \cite{drayson_detection_prevents_collapse}
…[truncated 2153 chars]

1	\section{Conclusion}
2	\label{sec:conclusion}
3	
4	Recursive generation in base models imprints a systematic distributional signature: central-tendency features drift with depth while shape features remain stable, a dichotomy we term differential depth response (DDR), validated across eight generators from five families.
5	This enables cross-generator depth classification (leak-free binary F1\,=\,0.694; end-to-end: 0.905) and provides an analytical lens complementary to model collapse: inference-chain drift compresses location while preserving shape, whereas retraining erodes both.
6	Current boundaries are instruction tuning (alignment compresses DDR structure while token-level signal persists; QLoRA 0.89--0.91) and frontier scale, where preliminary evidence suggests attenuated but persistent effects.
7

1	\section{Related Work}
2	\label{sec:related}
3	
4	\subsection{AI-Generated Text Detection}
5	\label{sec:related-detection}
6	
7	The detection literature spans statistical tests \cite{gltr,grover,release_strategies}, zero-shot scoring \cite{detectgpt,fast_detectgpt,binoculars,dna_gpt}, surprisal diversity \cite{diveye}, geometric invariants \cite{intrinsic_dim_detection}, and learned classifiers \cite{ghostbuster,feat_aug_transformer,reliable_detection,ld_score}; see \citet{wu_survey_2025} and \citet{jawahar_survey} for surveys.
8	RAID \cite{raid} benchmarks binary detection across generators and attacks; we extend the task from binary to ordinal depth estimation.
9	Several recent efforts move beyond the binary boundary (collaboration roles \cite{beyond_binary}, joint detection-attribution \cite{two_birds}, creator-editor modes \cite{beyond_final_actor}, multi-generator attribution \cite{semeval2024_task8}) but all taxonomize by \emph{source} or \emph{production mode}, not recursive generation \emph{depth}; repurposing their scores as depth predictors reveals high cross-generator variance (binary F1\,=\,0.52--0.71; Table~\ref{tab:baselines}) \cite{paraphrase_evades,detection_possibilities}.
10	Our contribution is orthogonal: ordinal depth classification \cite{gutierrez_ordinal_survey,diaz_soft_labels_ordinal,coral_ordinal,kasa_ordinality_nlp} as a task, and the finding that combining drifting and stationary features yields consistent cross-generator performance where individual detection scores show high variance \cite{uchendu_attribution,munir_looking_glass}.
11	
12	\subsection{Model Collapse and Iterative Generation}
13	\label{sec:related-collapse}
14	
15	Self-consuming generative models progressively lose distributional coverage \cite{self_consuming_mad,curse_of_recursion,briesch_self_consuming,seddik_synthetic_data}, though data mixing mitigates collapse \cite{model_collapse_avoidable,strong_model_collapse}.
16	These studies address \emph{training-loop} degrada
…[truncated 3040 chars]

1	\section*{Limitations}
2	\label{sec:limitations}
3	
4	All eight generators are base (pretrained) models; instruction-tuned variants show substantially degraded depth signal (Appendix~\ref{app:instruct}), and extending the method to RLHF-aligned models (which dominate real-world text) requires addressing confounds from length regulation and safety-driven distribution shaping.
5	Our largest generator is 8.2B parameters; frontier models (70B+) remain untested and their higher fluency may further compress feature margins.
6	
7	The evaluation assumes a fixed decoding regime (temperature 0.7, nucleus $p{=}0.9$); cross-temperature transfer degrades when test-time temperature exceeds training-time settings (Appendix~\ref{app:cross-temp}).
8	All seed texts are English Wikipedia excerpts, and the reference model (Pythia-410M) has English-centric pretraining, limiting non-English applicability.
9	
10	Depth resolution is bounded at three classes (gen-0\,/\,gen-1\,/\,gen-2+); finer-grained classification (5-class) yields F1\,$\approx$\,0.50 with depths 2--4 mutually indistinguishable (Appendix~\ref{app:finer-depth}).
11	Finally, our cross-model chain and simulated editing experiments (\S\ref{sec:discussion}) represent controlled laboratory settings; the gap to real-world post-processing pipelines remains untested.
12

  "new_string": "We formalize this asymmetry as \\emph{differential depth response} (DDR) across eight \\textbf{base-model} generators spanning five architectural families (0.6B--8B parameters).\nUnder nucleus sampling ($p{=}0.9$, $\\tau \\leq 0.7$), NLL location statistics and lexical diversity drift monotonically with depth (Cohen's $d{=}0.76$--$1.17$) while distributional shape features remain near-stationary ($d < 0.10$); clustering-based validation (silhouette\\,=\\,0.660) and convergent effect-size measures confirm this partition."
}
```

{
  "replace_all": "False",
  "file_path": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0514_3/projects/gen-depth-fingerprint/artifacts/_project/paper/introduction.tex",
  "old_string": "Yet this framing elides a richer structure emerging on the open web: as LLM outputs re-enter training corpora \\cite{dolma,fineweb} and seed further generation, text accumulates at varying \\emph{recursive depths}: an estimated 7--17\\% of recent scientific publications already contain substantial LLM-modified content \\cite{mapping_llm_science,monitoring_ai_review}, and each generation cycle pushes distributional properties further from both the human source and the first-generation predecessor.\nWhile most deployed LLM outputs use instruction-tuned models, base-model generation pipelines remain prevalent in synthetic data production, pre-training corpus construction, and recursive data augmentation; we accordingly scope this study to base (pretrained) models.\nKnowing \\emph{how many times} text has been recursively generated matters for data provenance \\cite{ccnet,dsir} and model collapse analysis \\cite{curse_of_recursion,strong_model_collapse}, yet no current method moves beyond the binary boundary.",
  "new_string": "Yet this framing elides a richer structure emerging on the open web: as LLM outputs re-enter training corpora \\cite{dolma,fineweb} and seed further generation, text accumulates at varying \\emph{recursive depths}, with an estimated 7--17\\% of recent scientific publications already containing substantial LLM-modified content \\cite{mapping_llm_science,monitoring_ai_review}.\nEach generation cycle pushes distributional properties further from both the human source and the first-generation predecessor.\nKnowing \\emph{how many times} text has been recursively generated matters for data provenance \\cite{ccnet,dsir} and model collapse analysis \\cite{curse_of_recursion,strong_model_collapse}, yet no current method moves beyond the binary boundary.\\footnote{We scope this study to base (pretrained) models, which remain prevalent in synthetic data production and corpus construction; instruction-tuned models are analyzed in Appendix~\\ref{app:instruct}.}"
}
```

_source line 4631 · 2026-05-21T11:23:28.477198+00:00_

called Edit

<details open><summary>🔧 Tool call — Edit</summary>

```json
{
  "replace_all": "False",
  "file_path": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0514_3/projects/gen-depth-fingerprint/artifacts/_project/paper/introduction.tex",
  "old_string": "Distributional compression under recursive generation is well-documented \\cite{curious_decline,broken_telephone,markovian_chains}.\nWe introduce \\emph{depth fingerprinting}, classifying text by ordinal generation depth (gen-0\\,/\\,gen-1\\,/\\,gen-2+), and decompose this compression into structurally distinct components: across eight base-model generators spanning five architectural families (0.6B--8B), the NLL distribution's central tendency (mean entropy, lexical diversity) drifts monotonically with depth while its shape (entropy extremes, tail structure) remains an order of magnitude more stable ($d{=}0.76$--$1.17$ vs.\\ $d < 0.10$; convergence across parametric and non-parametric effect-size measures confirms this partition).\nWe term this location/shape asymmetry the \\emph{differential depth response} (DDR; Figure~\\ref{fig:feature-drift}), a structural property of fixed-parameter autoregressive sampling that is mechanistically distinct from the tail-coverage erosion in model collapse \\cite{curse_of_recursion} and analytically informative independent of both the specific mechanism and classification accuracy.\nWhere concurrent work characterizes \\emph{that} and \\emph{why} convergence occurs \\cite{broken_telephone,markovian_chains}, DDR isolates \\emph{what} converges from what is preserved, and leverages the stationary shape component for cross-generator depth prediction without per-generator calibration.",
  "new_string": "Distributional compression under recursive generation is well-documented \\cite{curious_decline,broken_telephone,markovian_chains}, yet existing analyses characterize \\emph{that} and \\emph{why} convergence occurs without decomposing what drifts from what is preserved.\nWe introduce \\emph{depth fingerprinting}, classifying text by ordinal generation depth (gen-0\\,/\\,gen-1\\,/\\,gen-2+), and decompose this compression into structurally distinct components: across eight base-model generators spanning five architectural families (0.6B--8B), the NLL distribution's central tendency (mean entropy, lexical diversity) drifts monotonically with depth while its shape (entropy extremes, tail structure) remains an order of magnitude more stable ($d{=}0.76$--$1.17$ vs.\\ $d < 0.10$; convergent effect-size measures confirm this partition).\nWe term this location/shape asymmetry the \\emph{differential depth response} (DDR; Figure~\\ref{fig:feature-drift}), a structural property of fixed-parameter autoregressive sampling that is mechanistically distinct from the tail-coverage erosion in model collapse \\cite{curse_of_recursion}.\nThe stationary shape component provides the cross-generator reference needed for depth prediction without per-generator calibration."
}
```

</details>

[Director 指令] [Targeted Patch — Round 3] 针对剩余的 open review issues 做精准修复，同时继续参考已中稿 empirical 文章风格提升写作质量。EMNLP 2026 ARR，DDL 5/25。

**任务 1: Discussion §5 novelty positioning 加固（R32-N1 + R35-N2）**
Discussion L16 当前写法: "Prior work documents what converges...but not what is preserved; this complementary observation enables depth detection without per-generator calibration."

需要强化：DDR 的贡献不是发现新现象，而是对已知 distributional compression 做了精细的 location/shape 分解，产生了可操作的检测框架。与 Geng et al. (Markovian Chains) 的区分：他们证明 attractor dynamics 存在，我们分解 what drifts vs what is preserved 并利用这个分解做 cross-generator depth detection。用 1-2 句 tighten 这个区分。

**任务 2: Results §4.2 Validation paragraph 重构（R35-N1 partial）**
results.tex L80 当前 Validation paragraph 以 k=2 clustering 开头。改为以 per-generator split analysis 作为 primary validation（8 个 holdout generators 都独立恢复同一 partition），k=2 clustering 降为 secondary evidence。permutation test 保留。已在 Introduction Contribution 1 做了同步修改（"per-generator split analysis and convergent effect-size measures confirm"），Results 需与之一致。

**任务 3: Conclusion 增加一句 forward-looking impact（提分）**
当前 Conclusion 只有 3 句话，过于简短。在最后加一句前瞻性影响（如 DDR 框架为 recursive generation 的质量控制提供了可操作的诊断工具，或为 model collapse prevention 提供了新的监测维度），不超过 15 词。保持 conclusion 紧凑。

**硬约束（绝对不能违反）：**
1. Abstract 中 "We identify this asymmetry as" 不能改成 "We formalize"（已修复的 R37-W4）
2. Abstract 中 "per-generator validation and convergent effect-size measures confirm this partition" 不能改回 "clustering-based validation (silhouette=0.660)"（刚修复的 R35-N1）
3. 不能增加页数超过 8 页正文
4. 所有修改必须同步到 docs/paper/ 和 artifacts/_project/paper/ 两个副本
5. 这次不用验证引用

_source line 4678 · 2026-05-21T11:34:23.504066+00:00_

1	% Abstract — Depth Fingerprinting of Recursive LLM Generation Chains
2	\begin{abstract}
3	When base-model LLM outputs re-enter generation pipelines, each recursive step compresses \emph{where} text sits in distributional space while leaving \emph{how} it is shaped nearly unchanged.
4	We identify this asymmetry as \emph{differential depth response} (DDR) and characterize it across eight \textbf{base-model} generators spanning five architectural families (0.6B--8B parameters).
5	Under nucleus sampling ($p{=}0.9$, $\tau \leq 0.7$), NLL location statistics and lexical diversity drift monotonically with depth (Cohen's $d{=}0.76$--$1.17$) while distributional shape features remain near-stationary ($d < 0.10$); per-generator validation and convergent effect-size measures confirm this partition.
6	Prior work on iterative generation documents \emph{what converges}; the central finding is \emph{what is preserved}: shape invariance provides the cross-generator reference needed for depth estimation without per-generator calibration.
7	As a proof of concept, a lightweight 7-feature ensemble exploiting DDR achieves leak-free binary F1\,=\,0.694 (gen-1 vs.\ gen-2+, effective resolution: three depth classes) under leave-one-generator-out evaluation, surpassing individual baselines and matching multi-detector ensembles at $2.4\times$ lower cost; an end-to-end classifier recovers substantially more (F1\,=\,0.905, 6-gen LOGO), establishing the analytical framework, not the specific features, as the primary contribution.
8	Instruction tuning compresses DDR structure, bounding current applicability to base models, though end-to-end classifiers recover depth signal under alignment.
9	\end{abstract}
10

1	\section{Introduction}
2	\label{sec:introduction}
3	
4	AI text detection asks a single binary question, \emph{human or machine}, and recent methods answer it with increasing reliability \cite{detectgpt,fast_detectgpt,binoculars,ld_score}.
5	Yet this framing elides a richer structure emerging on the open web: as LLM outputs re-enter training corpora \cite{dolma,fineweb} and seed further generation, text accumulates at varying \emph{recursive depths}, with an estimated 7--17\% of recent scientific publications already containing substantial LLM-modified content \cite{mapping_llm_science,monitoring_ai_review}.
6	Each generation cycle pushes distributional properties further from both the human source and the first-generation predecessor.
7	Knowing \emph{how many times} text has been recursively generated matters for data provenance \cite{ccnet,dsir} and model collapse analysis \cite{curse_of_recursion,strong_model_collapse}, yet no current method moves beyond the binary boundary.\footnote{We scope this study to base (pretrained) models, which remain prevalent in synthetic data production and corpus construction; instruction-tuned models are analyzed in Appendix~\ref{app:instruct}.}
8	
9	\begin{figure}[t!]
10	\centering
11	\includegraphics[width=\columnwidth]{fig1_differential_response.pdf}
12	\caption{Differential depth response (DDR). Diamonds: cross-generator means; dots: per-generator values. Drifting features (blue; $|\rho| > 0.60$, $d{=}0.76$--$1.17$) show strong monotonic depth sensitivity; non-drifting features remain stable. Entropy std (wine; $d{=}0.55$) is an intermediate member; non-drifting features (amber; $d < 0.10$) show negligible drift. Entropy min ($|\rho|{=}0.40$ pooled; gen-0/gen-1 sensitive) and tail decay (TOST 4/8) are boundary features within this group.}
13	\label{fig:feature-drift}
14	\end{figure}
15

1	\section{Discussion}
2	\label{sec:discussion}
3	
4	Recursive generation alters \emph{where} the token-level surprise distribution sits but not \emph{how} it is shaped, a dichotomy with a mechanistic basis.
5	Under nucleus sampling, top-$p$ truncation preserves the functional form of the conditional distribution at each step; what changes is the \emph{input}: as the model conditions on increasingly self-typical outputs, expected NLL compresses monotonically.
6	This yields two testable predictions: (1)~increasing sampling randomness should weaken the location drift, and (2)~semantic-preservation constraints should attenuate it.
7	Both are confirmed: at $t{=}1.0$, location signal collapses ($\rho$: $-0.60 \to -0.002$; Appendix~\ref{app:sampling-sensitivity}); under elaboration prompts, drift weakens (F1\,=\,0.730 vs.\ 0.781; Appendix~\ref{app:prompt-type}).
8	Causal analyses provide supporting evidence: drifting features contribute $5.5\times$ more to classification, with 6/7 exhibiting significant Granger causality over the five-point chain, though the short series length (5 depths) limits formal causal inference (Appendix~\ref{app:ddr-causality}).
9	These results are established under nucleus sampling ($p{=}0.95$); shape features remain stable even at $\tau{=}1.0$ ($d < 0.15$; Appendix~\ref{app:ddr-temp-stability}), but verification under greedy and top-$k$ decoding remains open.
10	
11	The classifier is a proof-of-concept for corpus-level auditing, not per-document filtering: gen-1 per-class F1\,=\,0.63 is insufficient for individual flagging but supports batch-level contamination estimation (${\pm}3$--$4$\% at $n{=}1{,}000$; Appendix~\ref{app:error-analysis}).
12	The three-class resolution enables depth-stratified corpus curation (retaining gen-1 content while flagging recursively degraded material), a distinction invisible to binary classifiers.
13	Depth-aware filtering can serve as a prophylactic against model collapse \cite{drayson_detection_prevents_collapse}
…[truncated 2148 chars]

50	\subsection{Differential Depth Response}
51	\label{sec:results-differential}
52	
53	\begin{figure*}[t]
54	\centering
55	\includegraphics[width=\textwidth]{fig_ddr_trajectory.pdf}
56	\caption{Feature trajectories across generation depth. \textbf{Top row (drifting)}: entropy mean and hapax ratio decline monotonically across all eight generators ($|\rho| > 0.59$). \textbf{Bottom row (stationary)}: entropy max and tail decay remain flat ($|\rho| < 0.07$). Colored lines: individual generators; diamonds: cross-generator mean (blue for drifting, amber for stationary). This dichotomy defines the differential depth response (DDR).}
57	\label{fig:ddr-trajectory}
58	\end{figure*}
59	
60	Recursive generation does not affect all distributional properties uniformly.
61	Figure~\ref{fig:ddr-trajectory} exposes a consistent dichotomy across all eight generators: central-tendency features (entropy mean, TTR, hapax ratio) decline monotonically with depth, while shape features (entropy max, tail decay, entropy min) remain flat.
62	This asymmetry constitutes the \emph{differential depth response} (DDR).
63	The primary partition is binary: $k{=}2$ clustering in standardized $(|\rho|, d)$ space yields silhouette\,=\,0.660 (Appendix~\ref{app:ddr-validation}), cleanly separating three \emph{drifting} features ($|\rho| > 0.60$, $d{=}0.76$--$1.17$) from four \emph{non-drifting} ones ($d \leq 0.55$); higher $k$ degrades cluster quality ($k{=}3$: silhouette\,=\,0.532).
64	A domain-informed refinement resolves entropy std as a sole \emph{intermediate} member ($d{=}0.55$, $|\rho|{=}0.16$), distinct from the three near-stationary features ($d < 0.10$; CV shift $<$3\% vs.\ $>$15\% for drifting features).
65	Multiple effect-size measures corroborate this partition, establishing that the asymmetry reflects distributional structure rather than feature-selection artifact (Quantification below).
66

1	\section{Conclusion}
2	\label{sec:conclusion}
3	
4	Recursive generation in base models imprints a systematic distributional signature: central-tendency features drift with depth while shape features remain stable, a dichotomy we term differential depth response (DDR), validated across eight generators from five families.
5	This enables cross-generator depth classification (leak-free binary F1\,=\,0.694; end-to-end: 0.905) and provides an analytical lens complementary to model collapse: inference-chain drift compresses location while preserving shape, whereas retraining erodes both.
6	Current boundaries are instruction tuning, where alignment compresses DDR structure while token-level signal persists (QLoRA 0.89--0.91), and frontier scale, where preliminary evidence indicates attenuated but persistent effects.
7

_source line 4700 · 2026-05-21T11:36:04.970914+00:00_

15	DDR is mechanistically distinct from model collapse \cite{curse_of_recursion,strong_model_collapse}: retraining erodes tail coverage by updating weights, whereas inference-chain drift at fixed parameters shifts only location, leaving shape stable.
16	Prior work establishes \emph{that} convergence occurs, from attractor dynamics \cite{markovian_chains} to diversity loss \cite{curious_decline}, without decomposing what drifts from what is preserved.
17	DDR supplies this decomposition: location drift carries the depth signal while shape stability provides the cross-generator reference, enabling detection without per-generator calibration.
18	The decomposition itself, isolating which distributional properties carry cross-generator depth signal, constitutes the analytical contribution, independent of whether shape stability originates from sampling truncation or deeper autoregressive properties.

_source line 4702 · 2026-05-21T11:36:06.082240+00:00_

79	\paragraph{Validation.}
80	Per-generator split analysis provides the strongest validation: each of the eight hold-out generators independently recovers the identical drifting/non-drifting partition (per-generator silhouette range 0.505--0.760; Appendix~\ref{app:ddr-validation}).
81	$k{=}2$ clustering in standardized $(|\rho|, d)$ space corroborates this separation (silhouette\,=\,0.660), invariant across threshold choices $\varepsilon \in [0.10, 0.75]$.
82	Per-generator TOST equivalence testing ($\varepsilon{=}|d|{<}0.20$, following Cohen's~\citeyearpar{cohen1988statistical} conventional threshold; gen-1 vs.\ gen-2+) confirms near-stationarity for entropy min (8/8 pass) and entropy max (6/8); tail decay passes for only 4/8, with $|d|{=}0.10$--$0.22$ spanning the boundary.
83	A permutation test ($n{=}10{,}000$) establishes the three-vs-four split as non-random ($p{=}0.030$; fixed-label silhouette\,=\,0.660 vs.\ null mean\,=\,0.46); exhaustive enumeration of all $\binom{7}{3}{=}35$ possible splits ranks this partition first (Appendix~\ref{app:ddr-validation}).

_source line 4712 · 2026-05-22T05:29:13.821015+00:00_

1	% Abstract — Depth Fingerprinting of Recursive LLM Generation Chains
2	\begin{abstract}
3	LLM-generated text is increasingly recycled into training corpora---an estimated 7--17\% of recent scientific and peer-review content is now machine-produced \cite{mapping_llm_science,monitoring_ai_review}---yet detection methods pose only a binary human-or-machine question, ignoring \emph{generation depth}: how many recursive LLM steps a text has undergone.
4	We show that successive re-generation compresses \emph{where} text sits in distributional space while leaving \emph{how} it is shaped nearly unchanged, an asymmetry we term \emph{differential depth response} (DDR).
5	Across eight base-model generators spanning five families (0.6B--8B parameters) under nucleus sampling ($p{=}0.9$, $\tau \leq 0.7$), NLL location and lexical diversity drift monotonically with depth (Cohen's $d{=}0.76$--$1.17$) while core distributional shape features remain near-stationary ($d \leq 0.10$); per-generator validation, convergent effect sizes, and greedy-decoding replication confirm this partition.
6	Prior work on iterative generation documents \emph{what converges}; the central finding here is \emph{what is preserved}: shape invariance provides a cross-generator reference for depth estimation without per-generator calibration.
7	A lightweight 7-feature ensemble exploiting DDR achieves leak-free binary F1\,=\,0.694 (gen-1 vs.\ gen-2+) under leave-one-generator-out evaluation, matching multi-detector ensembles at $2.4\times$ lower cost; an end-to-end classifier reaches 3-class F1\,=\,0.905 (6-gen LOGO), establishing the analytical framework as the primary contribution.
8	Instruction tuning compresses DDR, bounding current applicability to base models.
9	\end{abstract}
10

1	\section{Introduction}
2	\label{sec:introduction}
3	
4	An estimated 7--17\% of recent scientific publications already contain substantial LLM-modified content \cite{mapping_llm_science,monitoring_ai_review}. As these outputs re-enter training corpora \cite{dolma,fineweb} and seed further generation, text accumulates at varying \emph{generation depths}; each successive cycle moves distributional properties further from both the human source and the first-generation predecessor. State-of-the-art detectors \cite{detectgpt,fast_detectgpt,binoculars,ld_score} answer only the binary question \emph{human or machine}, discarding the depth dimension that data provenance \cite{ccnet,dsir} and model collapse analysis \cite{curse_of_recursion,strong_model_collapse} increasingly require.\footnote{We scope this study to base (pretrained) models, which remain prevalent in synthetic data production and corpus construction; instruction-tuned models are analyzed in Appendix~\ref{app:instruct}.}
5	We find that recursive generation produces a decomposable fingerprint: distributional \emph{location} drifts with depth while \emph{shape} remains stable across generators, an asymmetry sufficient for cross-generator depth prediction.
6	
7	Distributional compression under recursive generation is well-documented \cite{curious_decline,broken_telephone,markovian_chains}, yet existing analyses characterize \emph{that} and \emph{why} convergence occurs without decomposing what drifts from what is preserved.
8	We introduce \emph{depth fingerprinting}, classifying text by ordinal generation depth (gen-0\,/\,gen-1\,/\,gen-2+), and decompose this compression into structurally distinct components: across eight base-model generators spanning five architectural families (0.6B--8B), the NLL distribution's central tendency (mean entropy, lexical diversity) drifts monotonically with depth while its shape (entropy extremes, tail structure) remains an order of magnitude more stable ($d{=}0.76$--$1.17$ vs.\
…[truncated 2140 chars]

1	\section{Discussion}
2	\label{sec:discussion}
3	
4	%% Mechanism — why DDR exists
5	The differential depth response arises from a structural property of nucleus sampling: top-$p$ truncation preserves the conditional distribution's functional form while recursive inputs grow increasingly self-typical, compressing expected NLL monotonically (Appendix~\ref{app:nll-distribution}; convergence trajectories in Appendix~\ref{app:convergence}).
6	Two testable predictions follow---increasing randomness should weaken location drift, semantic constraints should attenuate it---both confirmed: at $\tau{=}1.0$, depth--NLL correlation collapses to $\rho{=}{-}0.002$ (Appendix~\ref{app:sampling-sensitivity}); elaboration prompts reduce F1 to 0.730 (Appendix~\ref{app:prompt-type}); drifting features contribute $5.5\times$ more to classification with 6/7 showing Granger causality (Appendix~\ref{app:ddr-causality}).
7	Shape stability holds even when drift is suppressed: at $\tau{=}1.0$ ($d < 0.15$; Appendix~\ref{app:ddr-temp-stability}), under greedy decoding (mean $|d|{=}0.05$), and with top-$k$ ($k{=}50$; Appendix~\ref{app:decoding-strategy}).
8	
9	%% Implications — what DDR enables
10	Depth constitutes a new operational dimension for corpus management that binary human-vs-machine classifiers cannot access.
11	The classifier supports batch contamination estimation (${\pm}3$--$4$\% at $n{=}1{,}000$; gen-1 per-class F1\,=\,0.63; Appendix~\ref{app:error-analysis}) rather than per-document filtering; depth-stratified curation can also serve as a prophylactic against model collapse \cite{drayson_detection_prevents_collapse}, though downstream improvements remain equivalent to unfiltered baselines (TOST $\varepsilon{=}{\pm}2\%$; Appendix~\ref{app:downstream}).
12	
13	%% Scope \& Connections — where DDR works and fails
14	DDR is mechanistically distinct from model collapse \cite{curse_of_recursion,strong_model_collapse}: retraining erodes tail coverage by updating weights, whereas inference
…[truncated 759 chars]

1	\section{Conclusion}
2	\label{sec:conclusion}
3	
4	Recursive generation in base models imprints a differential depth response: central-tendency features drift with depth while shape features remain stable, enabling cross-generator depth classification (leak-free binary F1\,=\,0.694; end-to-end 3-class: 0.905) across eight generators from five families, replicated under deterministic decoding.
5	The decomposition establishes depth as an operational axis for data curation and collapse prevention, complementary to model-collapse analyses that target retraining-induced degradation.
6	More broadly, DDR suggests that fixed-parameter generation chains carry an inherent structural fingerprint persisting across architectures and scales; extending this signal to instruction-tuned models and provenance-aware data pipelines remains the key open challenge.
7

1	\section*{Limitations}
2	\label{sec:limitations}
3	
4	All eight generators are base (pretrained) models; instruction-tuned variants show substantially degraded depth signal (Appendix~\ref{app:instruct}), and extending the method to RLHF-aligned models (which dominate real-world text) requires addressing confounds from length regulation and safety-driven distribution shaping.
5	Our largest generator is 8.2B parameters; frontier models (70B+) remain untested and their higher fluency may further compress feature margins.
6	
7	The evaluation assumes a fixed decoding regime (temperature 0.7, nucleus $p{=}0.9$); cross-temperature transfer degrades when test-time temperature exceeds training-time settings (Appendix~\ref{app:cross-temp}).
8	All seed texts are English Wikipedia excerpts, and the reference model (Pythia-410M) has English-centric pretraining, limiting non-English applicability.
9	
10	Depth resolution is bounded at three classes (gen-0\,/\,gen-1\,/\,gen-2+); finer-grained classification (5-class) yields F1\,$\approx$\,0.50 with depths 2--4 mutually indistinguishable (Appendix~\ref{app:finer-depth}).
11	Our cross-model chain and simulated editing experiments (\S\ref{sec:discussion}) represent controlled laboratory settings; the gap to real-world post-processing pipelines remains untested.
12	Top-$k$ sampling preserves DDR for non-Pythia/OLMo generators but shows model-intrinsic boundary violations in Pythia and OLMo families; broader decoding-strategy coverage (e.g., beam search) remains future work.
13

1	\section{Results}
2	\label{sec:results}
3	
4	\subsection{Depth Classification Performance}
5	\label{sec:results-classification}
6	
7	The 7-feature ensemble achieves 3-class LOGO F1\,=\,0.762 (95\% CI [0.758, 0.766]; $N$\,=\,37{,}058) across eight generators from five families (Table~\ref{tab:logo-results}), with cross-generator F1 spread of 9.1\,pp capturing generator selection uncertainty.
8	Because gen-0 seeds are shared across generators (\S\ref{sec:generation}), gen-0 classification is near-perfect (per-class F1\,$\approx$\,0.94), inflating the 3-class metric.
9	We therefore adopt \textbf{leak-free binary F1} (gen-1 vs.\ gen-2+)\,=\,0.694 (CI [0.688, 0.700]) as the primary metric throughout: it excludes gen-0 entirely and is unaffected by seed sharing.
10	Per-generator binary F1 ranges from 0.633 (Mistral-7B) to 0.724 (Pythia-2.8B; SD\,=\,0.028), with the primary difficulty concentrated at the gen-1\,/\,gen-2+ boundary (Appendix~\ref{app:confusion}).
11	CIs reflect bootstrap sampling variability; the cross-generator range (9.1\,pp) captures the distinct and larger uncertainty of generator selection.
12	
13	\paragraph{Cross-model generalization.}
14	Cross-model chains (randomly selected generator per step) yield binary F1\,=\,0.830 (95\% CI [.818, .841]; Appendix~\ref{app:cross-model-detail}) under 5-generator LOGO.\footnote{Pythia-2.8B/1B, OLMo-2-1B, Qwen3-0.6B/1.7B; generator-level LOGO with bootstrap CIs ($n$\,=\,2{,}000). Not directly comparable to 8-generator LOGO due to differing subsets.}
15	Single-generator transfer (Appendix~\ref{app:transfer}) confirms the signal is not a mixed-chain artifact (mean transfer F1\,=\,0.810; LOFO\,=\,0.686).
16	A scale effect emerges: Pythia achieves the highest F1 (0.718--0.724) while larger models degrade (Mistral-7B: 0.633), partly reflecting same-family affinity with the Pythia-410M reference (cross-reference spread $\leq$0.013; \S\ref{sec:results-differential}); cross-domain signal persists with variation (Appendix~\
…[truncated 13165 chars]

_source line 4724 · 2026-05-22T05:32:19.728772+00:00_

25	\subsection{Task Formulation}
26	\label{sec:task}
27	
28	Given a text $x$, we predict its \emph{generation depth} $d \in \{0, 1, 2{+}\}$: human-written ($d{=}0$), first-generation LLM output ($d{=}1$), or text produced by two or more recursive LLM generations ($d{=}2{+}$).
29	A generation chain of depth $k$ is defined recursively: $x^{(0)} \sim \mathcal{C}_0$ (human corpus), $x^{(k)} = G(x^{(k-1)})$ for $k \geq 1$, where $G$ denotes stochastic autoregressive generation.
30	Depths beyond 2 are merged into $2{+}$ because feature convergence compresses inter-class margins at deeper levels (\S\ref{sec:results-differential}).
31	
32	\subsection{Generation Chain Construction}
33	\label{sec:generation}
34	
35	We construct generation chains using eight generators spanning five model families: \textbf{Pythia} (2.8B, 1B) \cite{pythia}, \textbf{OLMo} (OLMo-2-1B) \cite{olmo}, \textbf{Qwen} (Qwen3-1.7B, Qwen3-0.6B, Qwen3-8B-Base) \cite{qwen3}, \textbf{Llama} (Llama-3.2-3B) \cite{llama3}, and \textbf{Mistral} (Mistral-7B-v0.3) \cite{mistral7b}.
36	These generators span five distinct architectures with a 13$\times$ parameter range (0.6B--8B), trained on heterogeneous corpora, ensuring that observed depth patterns reflect properties of recursive generation rather than idiosyncrasies of any single model family or training distribution.
37	
38	For each generator, we sample approximately 1{,}000 seed passages from English Wikipedia as $\mathcal{C}_0$.
39	The same pool of seed passages serves as gen-0 for all generators.

1	Can depth-aware data curation improve downstream language model quality?
2	We fine-tune OLMo-2-0425-1B on five data variants (3 seeds each) and evaluate perplexity on a held-out test set (Table~\ref{tab:downstream}).
3	
4	\begin{table}[ht]
5	\centering
6	\small
7	\begin{tabular}{lrc}
8	\toprule
9	\textbf{Variant} & \textbf{$n$} & \textbf{Test PPL} \\
10	\midrule
11	All depths            & 12{,}299 & 2.335 $\pm$ 0.000 \\
12	Size-matched (random) & 3{,}951  & 2.367 $\pm$ 0.000 \\
13	Depth 0--1 only       & 3{,}951  & 2.387 $\pm$ 0.001 \\
14	Binary-filtered       & 3{,}951  & 2.389 $\pm$ 0.000 \\
15	Depth 0 only          & 797      & 2.681 $\pm$ 0.001 \\
16	\bottomrule
17	\end{tabular}
18	\caption{Downstream fine-tuning perplexity (OLMo-2-0425-1B, mean $\pm$ std across 3 seeds). At equal sample count, random subsampling (2.367) outperforms depth-aware curation (depth 0--1: 2.387, binary-filtered: 2.389).}
19	\label{tab:downstream}
20	\end{table}
21	
22	The results are unambiguous: data quantity dominates depth composition.
23	Training on all 12{,}299 samples yields the lowest perplexity (2.335).
24	At equal sample count ($n$\,=\,3{,}951), random subsampling (2.367) \emph{outperforms} both depth-stratified selection (depth 0--1: 2.387) and binary detection filtering (2.389).
25	Cross-seed variance is below 0.002 across all variants, which confirms the robustness of this null result.
26	
27	Restricting training to human-only text (depth 0, $n$\,=\,797) produces a sharp perplexity increase to 2.681, driven primarily by the 5$\times$ reduction in data volume rather than depth composition per se.
28	
29	\paragraph{Equivalence testing.}
30	We apply a two one-sided $t$-test (TOST) with a $\pm$2\% relative margin (equivalence bounds $\pm$0.047 on the ppl scale) to formally assess whether the depth-aware and random subsampling conditions produce practically indistinguishable perplexity.

_source line 4772 · 2026-05-22T06:56:38.617757+00:00_

1	% Abstract — Depth Fingerprinting of Recursive LLM Generation Chains
2	\begin{abstract}
3	LLM-generated text is increasingly recycled into training corpora---an estimated 7--17\% of recent scientific and peer-review content is now machine-produced \cite{mapping_llm_science,monitoring_ai_review}---yet detection methods pose only a binary human-or-machine question, ignoring \emph{generation depth}: how many recursive LLM steps a text has undergone.
4	We show that successive re-generation compresses \emph{where} text sits in distributional space while leaving \emph{how} it is shaped nearly unchanged, an asymmetry we term \emph{differential depth response} (DDR).
5	Across eight base-model generators spanning five families (0.6B--8B parameters) under nucleus sampling ($p{=}0.9$, $\tau \leq 0.7$), NLL location and lexical diversity drift monotonically with depth (Cohen's $d{=}0.76$--$1.17$) while core distributional shape features remain near-stationary ($d \leq 0.10$); per-generator validation, convergent effect sizes, and greedy-decoding replication confirm this partition.
6	Prior work on iterative generation documents \emph{what converges}; the central finding here is \emph{what is approximately preserved} (near-stationary, $d \leq 0.10$): shape invariance provides a cross-generator reference for depth estimation without per-generator calibration.
7	A lightweight 7-feature ensemble exploiting DDR achieves leak-free binary F1\,=\,0.694 (gen-1 vs.\ gen-2+) under leave-one-generator-out evaluation, matching multi-detector ensembles at ${\sim}2.4\times$ lower cost; an end-to-end classifier reaches 3-class F1\,=\,0.905 (6-gen LOGO), establishing the analytical framework as the primary contribution.
8	Instruction tuning compresses DDR, bounding current applicability to base models.
9	\end{abstract}
10

1	\section{Introduction}
2	\label{sec:introduction}
3	
4	An estimated 7--17\% of recent scientific publications already contain substantial LLM-modified content \cite{mapping_llm_science,monitoring_ai_review}. As these outputs re-enter training corpora \cite{dolma,fineweb} and seed further generation, text accumulates at varying \emph{generation depths}; each successive cycle moves distributional properties further from both the human source and the first-generation predecessor. State-of-the-art detectors \cite{detectgpt,fast_detectgpt,binoculars,ld_score} answer only the binary question \emph{human or machine}, discarding the depth dimension that data provenance \cite{ccnet,dsir} and model collapse analysis \cite{curse_of_recursion,strong_model_collapse} increasingly require.
5	We find that recursive generation produces a decomposable fingerprint: distributional \emph{location} drifts with depth while \emph{shape} remains stable across generators, an asymmetry sufficient for cross-generator depth prediction.
6	
7	Distributional compression under recursive generation is well-documented \cite{curious_decline,broken_telephone,markovian_chains}, yet existing analyses characterize \emph{that} and \emph{why} convergence occurs without decomposing what drifts from what is preserved.
8	We introduce \emph{depth fingerprinting}, classifying text by ordinal generation depth (gen-0\,/\,gen-1\,/\,gen-2+), and decompose this compression into structurally distinct components: across eight base-model generators spanning five architectural families (0.6B--8B), the NLL distribution's central tendency (mean entropy, lexical diversity) drifts monotonically with depth while its shape (entropy extremes, tail structure) remains an order of magnitude more stable ($d{=}0.76$--$1.17$ vs.\ $d < 0.10$ for near-stationary features; boundary features show intermediate stability; convergent effect-size measures confirm this partition).

1	\section{Discussion}
2	\label{sec:discussion}
3	
4	%% Mechanism — why DDR exists
5	We hypothesize that the differential depth response reflects a structural property of nucleus sampling: top-$p$ truncation preserves the conditional distribution's functional form while recursive inputs grow increasingly self-typical, compressing expected NLL monotonically (Appendix~\ref{app:nll-distribution}); a formal derivation remains future work.
6	Two testable predictions follow---increasing randomness should weaken location drift, semantic constraints should attenuate it---both confirmed: at $\tau{=}1.0$, depth--NLL correlation collapses ($\rho{=}{-}0.002$; Appendix~\ref{app:sampling-sensitivity}); elaboration prompts reduce F1 to 0.730 (Appendix~\ref{app:prompt-type}). Drifting features contribute $5.5\times$ more (Appendix~\ref{app:ddr-causality}).
7	Shape stability holds even when drift is suppressed: at $\tau{=}1.0$ ($d < 0.15$; Appendix~\ref{app:ddr-temp-stability}), under greedy decoding (mean $|d|{=}0.05$), and with top-$k$ ($k{=}50$; Appendix~\ref{app:decoding-strategy}).
8	
9	%% Implications — what DDR enables
10	Depth constitutes a new operational dimension for corpus management that binary human-vs-machine classifiers cannot access.
11	The classifier supports batch contamination estimation (${\pm}3$--$4$\% at $n{=}1{,}000$; gen-1 per-class F1\,=\,0.63; Appendix~\ref{app:error-analysis}) rather than per-document filtering; depth-stratified curation can also serve as a prophylactic against model collapse \cite{drayson_detection_prevents_collapse}, though no downstream improvement over unfiltered baselines is detected (TOST $\varepsilon{=}{\pm}2\%$; limited power with $n{=}3$ seeds; Appendix~\ref{app:downstream}).
12	
13	%% Scope \& Connections — where DDR works and fails
14	DDR is mechanistically distinct from model collapse \cite{curse_of_recursion,strong_model_collapse}: retraining erodes tail coverage by updating weights, whereas inference-chain drift at fixed par

1	\section{Conclusion}
2	\label{sec:conclusion}
3	
4	Recursive generation in base models imprints a differential depth response: central-tendency features drift with depth while shape features remain stable, enabling cross-generator depth classification (leak-free binary F1\,=\,0.694; end-to-end 3-class: 0.905) across eight generators from five families, replicated under deterministic decoding.
5	The decomposition establishes depth as an analytical axis for corpus provenance characterization, complementary to model-collapse analyses that target retraining-induced degradation.
6	DDR suggests that fixed-parameter generation chains carry an inherent structural fingerprint across architectures and scales; extending depth estimation to instruction-tuned models, where RLHF regularization strength may predict DDR attenuation, and to frontier-scale generators remains the key open challenge.
7

1	\section*{Limitations}
2	\label{sec:limitations}
3	
4	All eight generators are base (pretrained) models; instruction-tuned variants show substantially degraded depth signal (Appendix~\ref{app:instruct}), and extending the method to RLHF-aligned models (which dominate real-world text) requires addressing confounds from length regulation and safety-driven distribution shaping.
5	We hypothesize that RLHF's KL-regularization smooths depth-specific NLL patterns, while SFT format constraints override the natural distributional drift that DDR captures; testing SFT-only models (without RLHF) could isolate these factors.
6	Our largest generator is 8.2B parameters; preliminary evidence from Qwen3-14B-Base shows partial DDR preservation with attenuated correlations ($|\rho|$ decay 22--35\%; Appendix~\ref{app:9gen-scale}), but frontier models (70B+) remain untested.
7	
8	The evaluation assumes a fixed decoding regime (temperature 0.7, nucleus $p{=}0.9$); cross-temperature transfer degrades when test-time temperature exceeds training-time settings (Appendix~\ref{app:cross-temp}).
9	All seed texts are English Wikipedia excerpts, and the reference model (Pythia-410M) has English-centric pretraining, limiting non-English applicability.
10	
11	Depth resolution is bounded at three classes (gen-0\,/\,gen-1\,/\,gen-2+); finer-grained classification (5-class) yields F1\,$\approx$\,0.50 with depths 2--4 mutually indistinguishable (Appendix~\ref{app:finer-depth}).
12	Our cross-model chain and simulated editing experiments (\S\ref{sec:discussion}) represent controlled laboratory settings; the gap to real-world post-processing pipelines remains untested.
13	Top-$k$ sampling preserves DDR for non-Pythia/OLMo generators but shows model-intrinsic boundary violations in Pythia and OLMo families; broader decoding-strategy coverage (e.g., beam search) remains future work.
14

1	% Abstract — Depth Fingerprinting of Recursive LLM Generation Chains
2	\begin{abstract}
3	LLM-generated text is increasingly recycled into training corpora---an estimated 7--17\% of recent scientific and peer-review content is now machine-produced \cite{mapping_llm_science,monitoring_ai_review}---yet detection methods pose only a binary human-or-machine question, ignoring \emph{generation depth}: how many recursive LLM steps a text has undergone.
4	We show that successive re-generation compresses \emph{where} text sits in distributional space while leaving \emph{how} it is shaped nearly unchanged, an asymmetry we term \emph{differential depth response} (DDR).
5	Across eight base-model generators spanning five families (0.6B--8B parameters) under nucleus sampling ($p{=}0.9$, $\tau \leq 0.7$), NLL location and lexical diversity drift monotonically with depth (Cohen's $d{=}0.76$--$1.17$) while core distributional shape features remain near-stationary ($d \leq 0.10$); per-generator validation, convergent effect sizes, and greedy-decoding replication confirm this partition.
6	Prior work on iterative generation documents \emph{what converges}; the central finding here is \emph{what is approximately preserved} (near-stationary, $d \leq 0.10$): shape invariance provides a cross-generator reference for depth estimation without per-generator calibration.
7	A lightweight 7-feature ensemble exploiting DDR achieves leak-free binary F1\,=\,0.694 (gen-1 vs.\ gen-2+) under leave-one-generator-out evaluation, matching multi-detector ensembles at ${\sim}2.4\times$ lower cost; an end-to-end classifier reaches 3-class F1\,=\,0.905 (6-gen LOGO), establishing the analytical framework as the primary contribution.
8	Instruction tuning compresses DDR, bounding current applicability to base models.
9	\end{abstract}
10

1	\section{Introduction}
2	\label{sec:introduction}
3	
4	An estimated 7--17\% of recent scientific publications already contain substantial LLM-modified content \cite{mapping_llm_science,monitoring_ai_review}. As these outputs re-enter training corpora \cite{dolma,fineweb} and seed further generation, text accumulates at varying \emph{generation depths}; each successive cycle moves distributional properties further from both the human source and the first-generation predecessor. State-of-the-art detectors \cite{detectgpt,fast_detectgpt,binoculars,ld_score} answer only the binary question \emph{human or machine}, discarding the depth dimension that data provenance \cite{ccnet,dsir} and model collapse analysis \cite{curse_of_recursion,strong_model_collapse} increasingly require.
5	We find that recursive generation produces a decomposable fingerprint: distributional \emph{location} drifts with depth while \emph{shape} remains stable across generators, an asymmetry sufficient for cross-generator depth prediction.
6	
7	Distributional compression under recursive generation is well-documented \cite{curious_decline,broken_telephone,markovian_chains}, yet existing analyses characterize \emph{that} and \emph{why} convergence occurs without decomposing what drifts from what is preserved.
8	We introduce \emph{depth fingerprinting}, classifying text by ordinal generation depth (gen-0\,/\,gen-1\,/\,gen-2+), and decompose this compression into structurally distinct components: across eight base-model generators spanning five architectural families (0.6B--8B), the NLL distribution's central tendency (mean entropy, lexical diversity) drifts monotonically with depth while its shape (entropy extremes, tail structure) remains an order of magnitude more stable ($d{=}0.76$--$1.17$ vs.\ $d < 0.10$ for near-stationary features; boundary features show intermediate stability; convergent effect-size measures confirm this partition).
9	We term this location/shape asymmetry the \emph{differenti

1	\section{Discussion}
2	\label{sec:discussion}
3	
4	%% Mechanism — why DDR exists
5	We hypothesize that the differential depth response reflects a structural property of nucleus sampling: top-$p$ truncation preserves the conditional distribution's functional form while recursive inputs grow increasingly self-typical, compressing expected NLL monotonically (Appendix~\ref{app:nll-distribution}); a formal derivation remains future work.
6	Two testable predictions follow---increasing randomness should weaken location drift, semantic constraints should attenuate it---both confirmed: at $\tau{=}1.0$, depth--NLL correlation collapses ($\rho{=}{-}0.002$; Appendix~\ref{app:sampling-sensitivity}); elaboration prompts reduce F1 to 0.730 (Appendix~\ref{app:prompt-type}). Drifting features contribute $5.5\times$ more (Appendix~\ref{app:ddr-causality}).
7	Shape stability holds even when drift is suppressed: at $\tau{=}1.0$ ($d < 0.15$; Appendix~\ref{app:ddr-temp-stability}), under greedy decoding (mean $|d|{=}0.05$), and with top-$k$ ($k{=}50$; Appendix~\ref{app:decoding-strategy}).
8	
9	%% Implications — what DDR enables
10	Depth constitutes a new operational dimension for corpus management that binary human-vs-machine classifiers cannot access.
11	The classifier supports batch contamination estimation (${\pm}3$--$4$\% at $n{=}1{,}000$; gen-1 per-class F1\,=\,0.63; Appendix~\ref{app:error-analysis}) rather than per-document filtering; depth-stratified curation can also serve as a prophylactic against model collapse \cite{drayson_detection_prevents_collapse}, though no downstream improvement over unfiltered baselines is detected (TOST $\varepsilon{=}{\pm}2\%$; limited power with $n{=}3$ seeds; Appendix~\ref{app:downstream}).
12	
13	%% Scope \& Connections — where DDR works and fails
14	DDR is mechanistically distinct from model collapse \cite{curse_of_recursion,strong_model_collapse}: retraining erodes tail coverage by updating weights, whereas inference-chain drift at fixed par
…[truncated 734 chars]

1	\section{Conclusion}
2	\label{sec:conclusion}
3	
4	Recursive generation in base models imprints a differential depth response: central-tendency features drift with depth while shape features remain stable, enabling cross-generator depth classification (leak-free binary F1\,=\,0.694; end-to-end 3-class: 0.905) across eight generators from five families, replicated under deterministic decoding.
5	The decomposition establishes depth as an analytical axis for corpus provenance characterization, complementary to model-collapse analyses that target retraining-induced degradation.
6	DDR suggests that fixed-parameter generation chains carry an inherent structural fingerprint across architectures and scales; extending depth estimation to instruction-tuned models, where RLHF regularization strength may predict DDR attenuation, and to frontier-scale generators remains the key open challenge.
7

_source line 4796 · 2026-05-22T06:59:12.727040+00:00_

1	\section*{Limitations}
2	\label{sec:limitations}
3	
4	All eight generators are base (pretrained) models; instruction-tuned variants show substantially degraded depth signal (Appendix~\ref{app:instruct}), and extending the method to RLHF-aligned models (which dominate real-world text) requires addressing confounds from length regulation and safety-driven distribution shaping.
5	We hypothesize that RLHF's KL-regularization smooths depth-specific NLL patterns, while SFT format constraints override the natural distributional drift that DDR captures; testing SFT-only models (without RLHF) could isolate these factors.
6	Our largest generator is 8.2B parameters; preliminary evidence from Qwen3-14B-Base shows partial DDR preservation with attenuated correlations ($|\rho|$ decay 22--35\%; Appendix~\ref{app:9gen-scale}), but frontier models (70B+) remain untested.
7	
8	The evaluation assumes a fixed decoding regime (temperature 0.7, nucleus $p{=}0.9$); cross-temperature transfer degrades when test-time temperature exceeds training-time settings (Appendix~\ref{app:cross-temp}).
9	All seed texts are English Wikipedia excerpts, and the reference model (Pythia-410M) has English-centric pretraining, limiting non-English applicability.
10	
11	Depth resolution is bounded at three classes (gen-0\,/\,gen-1\,/\,gen-2+); finer-grained classification (5-class) yields F1\,$\approx$\,0.50 with depths 2--4 mutually indistinguishable (Appendix~\ref{app:finer-depth}).
12	Our cross-model chain and simulated editing experiments (\S\ref{sec:discussion}) represent controlled laboratory settings; the gap to real-world post-processing pipelines remains untested.
13	Top-$k$ sampling preserves DDR for non-Pythia/OLMo generators but shows model-intrinsic boundary violations in Pythia and OLMo families; broader decoding-strategy coverage (e.g., beam search) remains future work.
14

1	\section{Method}
2	\label{sec:method}
3	
4	\begin{table}[t!]
5	\centering
6	\footnotesize
7	\caption{Seven distributional features. Reference features use Pythia-410M per-token NLL; surface features require no reference model. DDR analysis (\S\ref{sec:results-differential}) reveals that entropy mean and the two surface features (TTR, hapax ratio) drift with depth while entropy max, min, and tail decay remain near-stationary; entropy std is intermediate.}
8	\label{tab:features}

11	\textbf{Feature} & \textbf{Source} & \textbf{Measures} \\
12	\midrule

14	entropy\_std  & Ref.\ NLL & Spread of token surprise \\
15	entropy\_max  & Ref.\ NLL & Maximum token surprise \\
16	entropy\_min  & Ref.\ NLL & Minimum token surprise \\
17	tail\_decay   & Ref.\ NLL & Log-prob decay slope (ranks 100--1K) \\

25	\subsection{Task Formulation}
26	\label{sec:task}
27	
28	Given a text $x$, we predict its \emph{generation depth} $d \in \{0, 1, 2{+}\}$: human-written ($d{=}0$), first-generation LLM output ($d{=}1$), or text produced by two or more recursive LLM generations ($d{=}2{+}$).
29	A generation chain of depth $k$ is defined recursively: $x^{(0)} \sim \mathcal{C}_0$ (human corpus), $x^{(k)} = G(x^{(k-1)})$ for $k \geq 1$, where $G$ denotes stochastic autoregressive generation.
30	Depths beyond 2 are merged into $2{+}$ because feature convergence compresses inter-class margins at deeper levels (\S\ref{sec:results-differential}).
31

## 修改清单（按优先级）

### P1: Introduction 重框 + Abstract 同步
**W-C2 + W-S9**

1. **Abstract 精简数字**：当前 8+ 数值结果，精简至 3-4 个关键指标（DDR effect sizes d=0.76-1.17 vs d≤0.10, leak-free binary F1=0.694, 3-class F1=0.905）。将 cost/parameter range/sampling params 等 context 信息移至 Introduction。

2. **Introduction §1 重构段落权重**：
   - 保留 "7-17% of scientific publications" stat（来源可靠：Liang et al. COLM 2024 / ICML 2024），但 **重框 motivation**：从 "这些内容需要检测" 改为 "大量 LLM 生成内容的存在使得理解递归生成如何改变文本分布特性成为重要科学问题"
   - 第二段重心从 "existing detectors fail" 转向 "prior work documents THAT convergence occurs (model collapse) but not WHAT drifts vs WHAT is preserved" — 这是本文的 gap
   - 确保 base-model scope delineation 保持显眼（当前已有正文句子，不要退回到 footnote）

### P2: 标题修改
**W-C1 + R39-N3**

标题改为：**"Depth Profiling: Classifying Recursive Generation Depth in Base-Model LLM Text"**

全文所有 "depth fingerprinting" / "fingerprinting" 引用同步改为 "depth profiling" / "profiling"。Abstract 首句确保包含 "coarse ordinal classification" 定位。

### P3: Entropy/NLL 术语 (Plan B+)
**W-S2 / R32-W3（Round 32 以来反复出现，本轮必须彻底解决）**

采用 B+ 方案（不全文重命名，但大幅提升 disclaimer 可见性）：
1. §3.3 Feature Extraction 第一句话改为 bold/emphasized disclaimer："**Throughout this paper, 'entropy' features refer to token-level negative log-likelihood (NLL) under a reference model (Pythia-410M), not Shannon entropy of the token distribution.**"

35	The per-generator F1 values for the original eight generators shift by at most $[-0.011, +0.002]$, indicating that the inclusion of Gemma data in training does not destabilize existing generalization patterns.
36	
37	\paragraph{Gemma-2-2B anomaly.}
38	Gemma-2-2B is the weakest held-out generator: 3-class F1\,=\,0.628, binary F1\,=\,0.440.
39	Per-class breakdown reveals a striking asymmetry: gen-0 F1\,=\,0.991 and gen-2+ F1\,=\,0.856, but gen-1 F1\,=\,0.036; the classifier almost entirely fails to identify first-generation Gemma continuations.
40	This is not a data-quality artifact: the Gemma-2-2B partition contains 4{,}957 instances with a balanced depth distribution ($\sim$990 per level).
41	A plausible explanation is that Gemma-2's first-generation continuations occupy a region of feature space highly similar to human-written text (gen-0), causing systematic misclassification of gen-1 as gen-0 or gen-2+.
42	This constitutes an architecture-specific generalization limitation rather than a systemic failure of the depth fingerprinting approach.
43	Several architectural differences may explain this behavior: (a)~Gemma-2's distinct attention mechanism and SentencePiece tokenizer with a larger vocabulary may produce first-generation continuations whose feature-space signature closely resembles human text, causing systematic gen-0/gen-1 confusion; (b)~this anomaly does not affect DDR's validity for the remaining eight generators, where per-generator F1 shifts by at most 0.011 upon Gemma inclusion.
44	
45	\paragraph{Scale effect.}
46	A clear parameter-count gradient emerges across the nine generators: three of four sub-1.7B generators achieve F1\,$>$\,0.81 (OLMo-2-1B at 0.773 is the exception), mid-range models (2--3B) cluster at 0.63--0.81, and large models (7B+) fall to 0.60--0.70.
47	Qwen3-8B (8.2B parameters) achieves 3-class F1\,=\,0.696 under the 9-gen split, compared to 0.701 in the class-balanced 8-gen evaluation; this small difference reflects the changed trai

55	\begin{enumerate}[nosep,leftmargin=*]
56	\item \textbf{Drifting dominance.} The $5.5\times$ permutation-importance gap confirms that drifting features (entropy\_mean, ttr, hapax\_ratio) are the primary depth signal carriers, consistent with the DDR partition derived from convergence analysis (\S\ref{sec:results-differential}).
57	
58	\item \textbf{Entropy\_min lacks causal direction.} Beyond its small effect size (F1 drop\,=\,0.019), entropy\_min is the only feature without significant Granger causality ($p$\,=\,0.375). This strengthens the near-stationary classification: the feature is neither marginally important nor temporally predictive.
59	
60	\item \textbf{Entropy\_max asymmetry.} Entropy\_max ranks fourth in permutation importance (F1 drop\,=\,0.070) but produces the largest degradation in the counterfactual intervention ($-$2.0\,pp), indicating that it provides unique signal not redundant with other features; its causal contribution exceeds its marginal importance.
61	
62	\item \textbf{Mistral heterogeneity.} In per-generator Granger tests, Mistral-7B shows non-significant hapax\_ratio and ttr ($p > 0.4$), though Fisher combination across generators remains significant. This generator-specific attenuation partly explains Mistral's lowest LOGO F1 (0.605) and suggests that DDR fingerprints are weaker in models with heavier repetition penalties.
63	\end{enumerate}

  "old_string": "LLM-generated text is increasingly recycled into training corpora---an estimated 7--17\\% of recent scientific and peer-review content is now machine-produced \\cite{mapping_llm_science,monitoring_ai_review}---yet detection methods pose only a binary human-or-machine question, ignoring \\emph{generation depth}: how many recursive LLM steps a text has undergone.\nWe show that successive re-generation compresses \\emph{where} text sits in distributional space while leaving \\emph{how} it is shaped nearly unchanged, an asymmetry we term \\emph{differential depth response} (DDR).\nAcross eight base-model generators spanning five families (0.6B--8B parameters) under nucleus sampling ($p{=}0.9$, $\\tau \\leq 0.7$), NLL location and lexical diversity drift monotonically with depth (Cohen's $d{=}0.76$--$1.17$) while core distributional shape features remain near-stationary ($d \\leq 0.10$); per-generator validation, convergent effect sizes, and greedy-decoding replication confirm this partition.\nPrior work on iterative generation documents \\emph{what converges}; the central finding here is \\emph{what is approximately preserved} (near-stationary, $d \\leq 0.10$): shape invariance provides a cross-generator reference for depth estimation without per-generator calibration.\nA lightweight 7-feature ensemble exploiting DDR achieves leak-free binary F1\\,=\\,0.694 (gen-1 vs.\\ gen-2+) under leave-one-generator-out evaluation, matching multi-detector ensembles at ${\\sim}2.4\\times$ lower cost; an end-to-end classifier reaches 3-class F1\\,=\\,0.905 (6-gen LOGO), establishing the analytical framework as the primary contribution.\nInstruction tuning compresses DDR, bounding current applicability to base models.",
  "new_string": "As LLM outputs re-enter training corpora and seed further generation, understanding how recursive generation reshapes text distributions becomes essential for data provenance and corpus curation---yet existing detectors answer only the binary human-or-machine question, ignoring \\emph{generation depth}.\nWe decompose distributional drift under recursive generation into location and shape components, revealing an asymmetry we term \\emph{differential depth response} (DDR): across eight base-model generators from five families, NLL location features drift monotonically with depth ($d{=}0.76$--$1.17$) while shape features remain near-stationary ($d \\leq 0.10$).\nPrior work documents \\emph{what converges}; the central finding here is \\emph{what is approximately preserved}: shape invariance provides a cross-generator reference that enables coarse ordinal depth classification (gen-0\\,/\\,gen-1\\,/\\,gen-2+) without per-generator calibration, achieving leak-free binary F1\\,=\\,0.694 under leave-one-generator-out evaluation.\nInstruction tuning compresses DDR, bounding current applicability to base models."
}

As these outputs re-enter training corpora \\cite{dolma,fineweb} and seed further generation, text accumulates at varying \\emph{generation depths}; each successive cycle moves distributional properties further from both the human source and the first-generation predecessor. State-of-the-art detectors \\cite{detectgpt,fast_detectgpt,binoculars,ld_score} answer only the binary question \\emph{human or machine}, discarding the depth dimension that data provenance \\cite{ccnet,dsir} and model collapse analysis \\cite{curse_of_recursion,strong_model_collapse} increasingly require.\nWe find that recursive generation produces a decomposable depth profile: distributional \\emph{location} drifts with depth while \\emph{shape} remains stable across generators, an asymmetry sufficient for cross-generator depth prediction.",
  "new_string": "An estimated 7--17\\% of recent scientific publications already contain substantial LLM-modified content \\cite{mapping_llm_science,monitoring_ai_review}. As these outputs re-enter training corpora \\cite{dolma,fineweb} and seed further generation, text accumulates at varying \\emph{generation depths}; each successive cycle reshapes distributional properties in ways that matter for data provenance \\cite{ccnet,dsir} and model collapse analysis \\cite{curse_of_recursion,strong_model_collapse}. Understanding \\emph{how} recursive generation alters text distributions---and which properties change versus which are preserved---is therefore a prerequisite for principled corpus curation, yet current methods address only binary human-or-machine detection \\cite{detectgpt,fast_detectgpt,binoculars,ld_score}.\nWe find that recursive generation produces a decomposable depth profile: distributional \\emph{location} drifts with depth while \\emph{shape} remains stable across generators, an asymmetry sufficient for cross-generator depth prediction."

1	\section{Introduction}
2	\label{sec:introduction}
3	
4	An estimated 7--17\% of recent scientific publications already contain substantial LLM-modified content \cite{mapping_llm_science,monitoring_ai_review}. As these outputs re-enter training corpora \cite{dolma,fineweb} and seed further generation, text accumulates at varying \emph{generation depths}; each successive cycle reshapes distributional properties in ways that matter for data provenance \cite{ccnet,dsir} and model collapse analysis \cite{curse_of_recursion,strong_model_collapse}. Understanding \emph{how} recursive generation alters text distributions---and which properties change versus which are preserved---is therefore a prerequisite for principled corpus curation, yet current methods address only binary human-or-machine detection \cite{detectgpt,fast_detectgpt,binoculars,ld_score}.
5	We find that recursive generation produces a decomposable depth profile: distributional \emph{location} drifts with depth while \emph{shape} remains stable across generators, an asymmetry sufficient for cross-generator depth prediction.
6	
7	Distributional compression under recursive generation is well-documented \cite{curious_decline,broken_telephone,markovian_chains}, yet existing analyses characterize \emph{that} and \emph{why} convergence occurs without decomposing what drifts from what is preserved.
8	We introduce \emph{depth profiling}, classifying text by ordinal generation depth (gen-0\,/\,gen-1\,/\,gen-2+), and decompose this compression into structurally distinct components: across eight base-model generators spanning five architectural families (0.6B--8B), the NLL distribution's central tendency (mean entropy, lexical diversity) drifts monotonically with depth while its shape (entropy extremes, tail structure) remains an order of magnitude more stable ($d{=}0.76$--$1.17$ vs.\ $d < 0.10$ for near-stationary features; boundary features show intermediate stability; convergent effect-size measures confirm this par
…[truncated 2190 chars]

1	Table~\ref{tab:iterative-comparison} compares this work with two concurrent studies on iterative LLM generation.
2	All three examine how repeated inference transforms text, but differ in chain operator, analytic lens, and downstream goal.
3	Our continuation-chain setting permits richer depth signal and enables the location/shape decomposition that defines DDR.
4

27	  & 7--8B (3 open + GPT-4o-mini)
